------------------------------------------------------------
File: openai_adapter.ex
------------------------------------------------------------
defmodule ACN.AI.Adapters.OpenAIAdapter do
  @moduledoc """
  Adapter for OpenAI-compatible APIs (e.g., DashScope's Qwen-Flash).
  Implements native function calling without Python transpilation.
  """
  @behaviour ACN.AI.ModelBehavior
  require Logger
  alias ACN.AI.ModelBehavior.Response

  # Match Ollama timeout for consistency
  @default_opts [receive_timeout: 300_000]

  @impl true
  def chat(messages, tools, opts \\ []) do
    config = config()
    req_opts = Keyword.merge(@default_opts, opts)

    # Validate API key
    api_key = config[:api_key]
    if is_nil(api_key) or api_key == "" do
      Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] Missing API key. Set DASHSCOPE_API_KEY environment variable.")
      {:error, :missing_api_key}
    else
      do_chat(api_key, messages, tools, config, req_opts)
    end
  end

  defp do_chat(api_key, messages, tools, config, req_opts) do

    # Build request payload
    payload = build_request_payload(messages, tools, config, req_opts)
    
    # Debug logging
    log_request(payload, tools)

    # Make API call
    headers = [
      {"authorization", "Bearer #{api_key}"},
      {"content-type", "application/json"}
    ]

    endpoint = "#{config[:base_url]}/chat/completions"
    
    Logger.info("[OpenAIAdapter] Calling #{endpoint} (Timeout: #{req_opts[:receive_timeout]}ms)...")

    case Req.post(endpoint,
           json: payload,
           headers: headers,
           receive_timeout: req_opts[:receive_timeout],
           finch: ACN.HTTPClient) do
      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: 200, body: body}} ->
        Logger.info("[OpenAIAdapter] Successfully received response.")
        parse_response(body)

      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: 401, body: body}} ->
        Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] Authentication failed. Invalid API key.")
        {:error, {:auth_error, body}}

      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: 429, body: body}} ->
        Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] Rate limit exceeded.")
        {:error, {:rate_limited, body}}

      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: 503, body: body}} ->
        Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] Service unavailable.")
        {:error, {:service_unavailable, body}}

      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: status, body: body}} ->
        Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] API Error: #{status}")
        {:error, {:api_error, status, body}}

      {:error, %Mint.TransportError{reason: :timeout}} ->
        Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] Request timeout. Model may be taking too long.")
        {:error, :timeout}

      {:error, reason} ->
        Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] Network error: #{inspect(reason)}")
        {:error, reason}
    end
  end

  @impl true
  def embed(_text, _opts), do: {:error, :not_implemented}

  # --- Private Functions ---

  defp config do
    Application.get_env(:acn, :ai, [])[:openai] ||
      [
        base_url: "https://dashscope-intl.aliyuncs.com/compatible-mode/v1",
        model: "qwen-flash"
      ]
  end

  @doc false
  def build_request_payload(messages, tools, config, opts) do
    base_payload = %{
      # Enable internal reasoning output for Qwen (DashScope)
      "enable_thinking" => opts[:enable_thinking] || config[:enable_thinking] || true,
      model: opts[:model] || config[:model] || "qwen-flash",
      messages: messages,
      temperature: opts[:temperature] || 1
    }

    # Add tools if provided
    if tools != nil and tools != [] do
      Map.merge(base_payload, %{
        tools: wrap_tools(tools),
        tool_choice: "auto"
      })
    else
      base_payload
    end
  end

  @doc false
  def wrap_tools(tools) do
    Enum.map(tools, fn tool ->
      %{
        "type" => "function",
        "function" => %{
          "name" => tool["name"] || tool[:name],
          "description" => tool["description"] || tool[:description],
          "parameters" => tool["parameters"] || tool[:parameters]
        }
      }
    end)
  end

  @doc false
  def parse_response(body) do
    choice = List.first(body["choices"])
    
    if is_nil(choice) do
      Logger.error("[OpenAIAdapter] No choices in response: #{inspect(body)}")
      {:error, :invalid_response}
    else
      message = choice["message"]
      content = message["content"]
      thinking = message["reasoning_content"] || message["thought"]
      tool_calls = message["tool_calls"]
      finish_reason = choice["finish_reason"]

      # Debug logging
      log_response(content, tool_calls, thinking)

      # Build response struct
      response = %Response{
        content: content,
        thinking: thinking,
        tool_calls: tool_calls,
        finish_reason: finish_reason,
        token_usage: parse_token_usage(body)
      }

      {:ok, response}
    end
  end

  defp parse_token_usage(body) do
    usage = body["usage"]
    
    if usage do
      %{
        prompt_tokens: usage["prompt_tokens"] || 0,
        completion_tokens: usage["completion_tokens"] || 0,
        total: usage["total_tokens"] || 0
      }
    else
      %{total: 0}
    end
  end

  defp log_request(payload, tools) do
    IO.puts("\n\n🟢 [OpenAI Input] ================================================")
    IO.puts("Model: #{payload.model}")
    IO.puts("Endpoint: DashScope (Qwen-Flash)")
    
    if tools != nil and tools != [] do
      tool_names = Enum.map_join(tools, ", ", &(&1["name"] || &1[:name]))
      IO.puts("✅ Function Calling Enabled: #{length(tools)} tools (#{tool_names})")
    else
      IO.puts("⚠️ No tools provided")
    end

    IO.puts("--- Messages Sent (#{length(payload.messages)}) ---")
    Enum.each(payload.messages, fn msg ->
      role = String.upcase(msg[:role] || msg["role"] || "UNKNOWN")
      content = msg[:content] || msg["content"] || ""
      IO.puts("[#{role}]: #{content}")
    end)
    IO.puts("==================================================================\n")
  end

  defp log_response(content, tool_calls, thinking) do
    IO.puts("\n\n🟣 [OpenAI Output] ===============================================")
    
    if thinking && thinking != "" do
      IO.puts("💭 Reasoning:")
      IO.puts(thinking)
      IO.puts("------------------------------------------------------------------")
    end

    if tool_calls && length(tool_calls) > 0 do
      IO.puts("🔧 Tool Calls Detected (#{length(tool_calls)}):")
      Enum.each(tool_calls, fn call ->
        func = call["function"]
        IO.puts("  - #{func["name"]}(#{func["arguments"]})")
      end)
    end
    
    if content && content != "" do
      IO.puts("📝 Content:")
      IO.puts(content)
    end
    
    IO.puts("==================================================================\n")
  end
end


------------------------------------------------------------
File: ollama_adapter.ex
------------------------------------------------------------
defmodule ACN.AI.Adapters.OllamaAdapter do
  @moduledoc """
  Bridges ACN to an external Ollama instance hosting Tiny-Agent.
  Reuses the "Broca's Area" transpiler logic to speak Python to the model.
  """
  @behaviour ACN.AI.ModelBehavior
  require Logger
  alias ACN.AI.ModelBehavior.Response

  # CHANGE 1: Set timeout to 300s (Evidence: Generation takes ~142s on local hardware)
  @default_opts [receive_timeout: 300_000]

  @impl true
  def chat(messages, tools, opts \\ []) do
    config = config()
    req_opts = Keyword.merge(@default_opts, opts)

    # 1. Inbound Translation (Prepare Messages)
    # Unlike Nx.Serving which takes a raw string, Ollama takes a list of messages.
    # But we still need to inject the Python tool definitions into the system prompt.
    prepared_messages = prepare_messages_for_ollama(messages, tools)

    payload = %{
      model: req_opts[:model] || config[:model],
      messages: prepared_messages,
      stream: false, # Stable non-streaming mode
      options: %{
        temperature: req_opts[:temperature] || 0.1, # Low temp for code
        num_ctx: 4096, # Ensure high context
        # CHANGE 2: Force the model to stop if it tries to generate a User reply or end tag
        stop: ["<|im_end|>", "<|im_start|>", "User:", "Observation:"]
      }
    }

    # --- DEBUG VISIBILITY START ---
    IO.puts("\n\n🟢 [Ollama Input] ================================================")
    IO.puts("Model: #{payload.model}")
    
    # Check if Transpiler injected tools into System Prompt (First message)
    system_msg = List.first(prepared_messages)
    if String.contains?(system_msg[:content], "@tool") do
      IO.puts("✅ TRANSPILER ACTIVE: Python Tool Definitions detected in System Prompt.")
    else
      IO.puts("⚠️ TRANSPILER INACTIVE: No tools found in System Prompt.")
    end

    IO.puts("--- Full Context Sent ---")
    Enum.each(prepared_messages, fn msg -> 
      IO.puts("[#{String.upcase(msg.role)}]: #{msg.content}") 
    end)
    IO.puts("==================================================================\n")
    # --- DEBUG VISIBILITY END ---

    Logger.info("[Ollama] Reaching out to #{config[:base_url]} (Timeout: #{req_opts[:receive_timeout]}ms)...")

    case Req.post("#{config[:base_url]}/api/chat", 
      json: payload, 
      receive_timeout: req_opts[:receive_timeout],
      finch: ACN.HTTPClient) do
      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: 200, body: body}} ->
        Logger.info("[Ollama] successfully reached and response received.")
        content = body["message"]["content"]
        
        # --- DEBUG VISIBILITY START ---
        IO.puts("\n\n🟣 [Ollama Output] ===============================================")
        IO.puts(content)
        IO.puts("==================================================================\n")
        # --- DEBUG VISIBILITY END ---

        case parse_python_to_tool_calls(content) do
          {:tool_calls, tool_calls} ->
            {:ok, %Response{
              content: content,
              tool_calls: tool_calls,
              finish_reason: "tool_calls",
              token_usage: %{total: 0}
            }}

          {:text, text} ->
            {:ok, %Response{
              content: text,
              tool_calls: nil,
              finish_reason: "stop",
              token_usage: %{total: 0}
            }}
        end

      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: status, body: body}} ->
        Logger.error("[Ollama] API Error: #{status}")
        {:error, {:ollama_error, status, body}}

      {:error, %Mint.TransportError{reason: :timeout}} ->
        Logger.error("[Ollama] TIMEOUT: Model took too long to generate. It may be hallucinating.")
        {:error, :timeout}

      {:error, reason} ->
        Logger.error("[Ollama] Network Error: #{inspect(reason)}")
        {:error, reason}
    end
  end

  @impl true
  def embed(_text, _opts), do: {:error, :not_implemented}

  # --- Configuration ---
  defp config do
    Application.get_env(:acn, :ai, [])[:ollama] || 
      [base_url: "http://localhost:11434", model: "qwen3:0.6b"]
  end

  # --- Inbound Translation ---

  defp prepare_messages_for_ollama(messages, tools) do
    # 1. Build the Python Tool Definitions String
    python_tools = build_pythonic_tools(tools)

    # 2. Inject into System Prompt
    # We find the system message, append logic, or create one.
    {systems, others} = Enum.split_with(messages, &(&1[:role] == "system" or &1["role"] == "system"))
    
    system_content = 
      case systems do
        [] -> "You are a helpful AI assistant."
        msgs -> Enum.map_join(msgs, "\n", &(&1[:content] || &1["content"]))
      end

    full_system = system_content <> "\n\n" <> python_tools
    Logger.debug("[OllamaAdapter] Injected System Prompt (Start): #{String.slice(full_system, 0, 200)}...")
    Logger.debug("[OllamaAdapter] Injected Tools: #{Enum.map_join(tools, ", ", &(&1["name"] || &1[:name]))}")

    # 3. Map "tool" roles to "user" (Ollama might support tool roles but we simulate Python env)
    mapped_others = Enum.map(others, &map_role_and_content/1)

    [%{role: "system", content: full_system} | mapped_others]
  end

  defp map_role_and_content(msg) do
    role = msg[:role] || msg["role"]
    content = msg[:content] || msg["content"]

    case role do
      "tool" ->
        # Map tool outputs to user messages so the model sees them as feedback
        %{role: "user", content: "Tool Output: #{content}"}

      "assistant" ->
        calls = msg[:tool_calls] || msg["tool_calls"]

        if is_list(calls) do
          new_calls =
            Enum.map(calls, fn call ->
              case call["function"]["arguments"] do
                bin when is_binary(bin) ->
                  # Decode string back to map for Ollama API
                  new_args =
                    case Jason.decode(bin) do
                      {:ok, map} -> map
                      _ -> bin
                    end

                  put_in(call, ["function", "arguments"], new_args)

                _ ->
                  call
              end
            end)

          %{role: "assistant", content: content, tool_calls: new_calls}
        else
          %{role: "assistant", content: content}
        end

      _ ->
        %{role: role, content: content}
    end
  end

  defp build_pythonic_tools([]), do: ""
  defp build_pythonic_tools(tools) do
    tools_str =
      tools
      |> Enum.map_join("\n\n", fn tool ->
        name = tool["name"] || tool[:name]
        desc = tool["description"] || tool[:description]
        params = tool["parameters"] || tool[:parameters] || %{}
        # Handle cases where parameter structure varies slightly
        props = params["properties"] || params[:properties] || %{}
        args = Map.keys(props) |> Enum.join(", ")

        "@tool\ndef #{name}(#{args}):\n    '''#{desc}'''\n    pass"
      end)
    
    """
    You have access to the following Python tools. 
    
    #{tools_str}
    
    To use a tool, you must output a valid Python function call within a code block.
    Example:
    ```python
    tool_name(arg1="value", arg2="value")
    ```
    DO NOT define functions. ONLY call them.
    """
  end

  # --- Outbound Translation (The Python Parser - COPIED from TinyAgentAdapter) ---

  defp parse_python_to_tool_calls(text) do
    # 1. Extract ALL Python Blocks
    blocks = Regex.scan(~r/```(?:python)?\s*(.*?)\s*```/s, text)
    
    if blocks == [] do
      # Fallback: Check if the whole text looks like it might contain a tool call (for lazy models)
      if String.contains?(text, "(") and String.contains?(text, ")") do
         parse_code(text)
      else
         {:text, text}
      end
    else
      # Combine all Python blocks
      combined_code = Enum.map_join(blocks, "\n", fn [_, code] -> code end)
      parse_code(combined_code)
    end
  end

  defp parse_code(code) do
    # Split multi-line function calls into individual complete calls
    # then parse each one
    complete_calls = extract_complete_calls(code)
    
    tool_calls = 
      complete_calls
      |> Enum.map(&parse_single_call/1)
      |> Enum.filter(&(&1 != nil))
    
    if tool_calls == [] do
      Logger.debug("[OllamaAdapter] No tool calls extracted from code block.")
      {:text, code}
    else
      Logger.info("[OllamaAdapter] Extracted #{length(tool_calls)} tool calls.")
      {:tool_calls, tool_calls}
    end
  end

  # Extract complete function calls (handles multi-line)
  defp extract_complete_calls(code) do
    lines = String.split(code, "\n")
    
    # Accumulate complete function calls
    {calls, _} = 
      Enum.reduce(lines, {[], nil}, fn line, {calls, current} ->
        trimmed = String.trim(line)
        
        cond do
          # Skip empty lines and comments
          trimmed == "" || String.starts_with?(trimmed, "#") || String.starts_with?(trimmed, "def") ->
            {calls, current}
          
          # Start of new function call
          String.match?(trimmed, ~r/^(?:\w+\s*=\s*)?\w+\(/) && current == nil ->
            # Check if it's a complete one-liner
            if String.contains?(trimmed, ")") do
              {[trimmed | calls], nil}
            else
              # Start of multi-line call
              {calls, trimmed}
            end
          
          # Continuation of multi-line call
          current != nil ->
            new_current = current <> " " <> trimmed
            # Check if this line closes the call
            if String.contains?(trimmed, ")") do
              {[new_current | calls], nil}
            else
              {calls, new_current}
            end
          
          true ->
            {calls, current}
        end
      end)
    
    Enum.reverse(calls)
  end

  # Parse a single complete function call (may be from multiple lines)
  defp parse_single_call(call_str) do
    # Extract function name and arguments from complete call string
    case Regex.run(~r/^(?:\w+\s*=\s*)?(\w+)\((.*)\)/, String.trim(call_str)) do
      [_, name, args_str] ->
        # Try named args first, fallback to positional if valid
        arguments = 
          case parse_args_string(args_str) do
            args when map_size(args) > 0 -> args
            _ -> parse_positional_args(name, args_str)
          end
        
        %{
          "id" => UUID.uuid4(),
          "type" => "function",
          "function" => %{
            "name" => name,
            "arguments" => Jason.encode!(arguments) 
          }
        }
      _ -> nil
    end
  end

  defp parse_args_string(str) do
    # Regex supports: triple-double, triple-single, double, single quotes, and basic lists.
    # Group 1: Key
    # Group 2: Triple-Double Value
    # Group 3: Triple-Single Value
    # Group 4: Double Value
    # Group 5: Single Value
    # Group 6: List Value [...]
    
    # IMPROVEMENT: Use non-greedy match for lists if followed by a comma and another key-value pair, 
    # or just be careful with the greedy dot.
    Regex.scan(~r/(\w+)\s*=\s*(?:"""(.*?)"""|'''(.*?)'''|"([^"]*)"|'([^']*)'|(\[.*?\]))(?:\s*,|$)/s, str)
    |> Map.new(fn match ->
        case match do
          [_, key, val, "", "", "", ""] -> {key, val} # Triple Double
          [_, key, "", val, "", "", ""] -> {key, val} # Triple Single
          [_, key, "", "", val, "", ""] -> {key, val} # Double
          [_, key, "", "", "", val, ""] -> {key, val} # Single
          [_, key, "", "", "", "", list_str] -> 
             # Robust list parsing: extract elements between quotes
             # Handles escaped quotes and ignores internal mismatched quotes by looking for delimiters
             elements = 
               Regex.scan(~r/(?:"((?:\\.|[^"])*)"|'((?:\\.|[^'])*)')\s*(?:,|$|\])/, list_str)
               |> Enum.map(fn 
                 [_, "", v] -> v # Single quote match
                 [_, v, ""] -> v # Double quote match
                 [_, v] -> v     # Single capture case (if only one kind of quote present)
                 _ -> nil
               end)
               |> Enum.reject(&is_nil/1)
             
             # Filter out empty or merely whitespace strings
             clean_elements = Enum.filter(elements, fn v -> String.trim(v) != "" end)
             
             {key, clean_elements}
          # Fallback
          [_, key | captures] -> 
             value = Enum.find(captures, &(&1 != "")) || ""
             {key, value}
        end
    end)
  end


  defp parse_positional_args("worksheet_write", args_str) do
    # Support: "file", "content" | 'file', 'content' | var1, var2 | "file", var2
    # This is a bit looser to handle model's unquoted variables as strings
    case Regex.run(~r/['"]?(.*?)['"]?\s*,\s*['"]?(.*?)['"]?/s, String.trim(args_str)) do
      [_, filename, content] -> 
        %{"filename" => String.trim(filename), "content" => String.trim(content)}
      _ -> %{}
    end
  end

  defp parse_positional_args("submit_legislation", args_str) do
    # Robust element extraction handles nested quotes and escapes
    elements = 
      Regex.scan(~r/(?:"((?:\\.|[^"])*)"|'((?:\\.|[^'])*)')\s*(?:,|$|\])/, args_str)
      |> Enum.map(fn 
        [_, "", v] -> v # Single quote match
        [_, v, ""] -> v # Double quote match
        [_, v] -> v     # Single capture case
        _ -> nil
      end)
      |> Enum.reject(&is_nil/1)

    %{"rules" => elements}
  end

  defp parse_positional_args("submit_mediation", args_str) do
    parse_positional_args("submit_legislation", args_str)
  end

  defp parse_positional_args("submit_execution_plan", args_str) do
    # Try to parse as list of lists
    json_friendly = String.replace(args_str, "'", "\"")
    case Jason.decode(json_friendly) do
      {:ok, batches} when is_list(batches) -> %{"batches" => batches}
      _ -> %{}
    end
  end

  defp parse_positional_args("link_dependency", args_str) do
    # Matches: "A", "B" or 'A', 'B'
    case Regex.run(~r/['"](.*?)['"]\s*,\s*['"](.*?)['"]/s, args_str) do
      [_, p, c] -> %{"provider_task_id" => p, "consumer_task_id" => c}
      _ -> %{}
    end
  end

  defp parse_positional_args("set_node_identity", args_str) do
    # Matches: "LEAF" or "INTERMEDIATE" or 'LEAF' etc.
    case Regex.run(~r/['"](.*?)['"]/, args_str) do
      [_, identity] -> %{"identity" => identity}
      _ -> %{}
    end
  end

  defp parse_positional_args("query_wisdom", args_str) do
    # Matches first argument as query_text: "Query" or 'Query'
    case Regex.run(~r/['"](.*?)['"]/, args_str) do
      [_, query] -> %{"query_text" => query}
      _ -> %{}
    end
  end

  defp parse_positional_args(_, _), do: %{}
end


------------------------------------------------------------
File: gemini_adapter.ex
------------------------------------------------------------
defmodule ACN.AI.Adapters.GeminiAdapter do
  @moduledoc """
  Adapter for Google Gemini API.
  Implements native function calling without Python transpilation.
  """
  @behaviour ACN.AI.ModelBehavior
  require Logger
  alias ACN.AI.ModelBehavior.Response

  # Match other adapters timeout for consistency
  @default_opts [receive_timeout: 300_000]

  @impl true
  def chat(messages, tools, opts \\ []) do
    config = config()
    req_opts = Keyword.merge(@default_opts, opts)

    # Validate API key
    api_key = config[:api_key] || System.get_env("GEMINI_API_KEY") || "AQ.Ab8RN6Lr5M5XvcIqEmu3GjrdYW7EfrpZcacvv5NdMiOqYJdV6Q"
    if is_nil(api_key) or api_key == "" do
      Logger.error("[GeminiAdapter] Missing API key. Set GEMINI_API_KEY environment variable.")
      {:error, :missing_api_key}
    else
      do_chat(api_key, messages, tools, config, req_opts)
    end
  end

  defp do_chat(api_key, messages, tools, config, req_opts) do
    # Build request payload
    payload = build_request_payload(messages, tools, config, req_opts)
    
    # Debug logging
    log_request(payload, tools, config)

    # Make API call - PRIORITIZE RUNTIME OPTS
    model = req_opts[:model] || config[:model] || "gemini-3.1-flash-lite-preview"
    endpoint = "https://aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1/publishers/google/models/#{model}:generateContent?key=#{api_key}"
    
    headers = [
      {"content-type", "application/json"}
    ]

    Logger.info("[GeminiAdapter] Calling #{endpoint} (Timeout: #{req_opts[:receive_timeout]}ms)...")

    make_request_with_retry(endpoint, [json: payload, headers: headers, receive_timeout: req_opts[:receive_timeout], finch: ACN.HTTPClient], 0)
    |> parse_response()
  end

  defp make_request_with_retry(endpoint, req_params, retry_count) do
    case Req.post(endpoint, req_params) do
      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: 200, body: body}} ->
        Logger.info("[GeminiAdapter] Successfully received response.")
        body

      {:ok, %Req.Response{status: status, body: body}} ->
        if retry_count < 3 do
          Logger.error("[GeminiAdapter] API Error (Status: #{status}). Retrying in 5000ms... (Attempt #{retry_count + 1})")
          Process.sleep(5000)
          make_request_with_retry(endpoint, req_params, retry_count + 1)
        else
          Logger.error("[GeminiAdapter] API Error (Status: #{status}). Max retries reached. Body: #{inspect(body)}")
          {:error, {:api_error, status, body}}
        end

      {:error, reason} ->
        if retry_count < 3 do
          Logger.error("[GeminiAdapter] Network/Timeout Error: #{inspect(reason)}. Retrying in 5000ms... (Attempt #{retry_count + 1})")
          Process.sleep(5000)
          make_request_with_retry(endpoint, req_params, retry_count + 1)
        else
          Logger.error("[GeminiAdapter] Network/Timeout Error: #{inspect(reason)}. Max retries reached.")
          {:error, {:network_error, reason}}
        end
    end
  end

  @impl true
  def embed(_text, _opts), do: {:error, :not_implemented}

  # --- Private Functions ---

  defp config do
    Application.get_env(:acn, :ai, [])[:gemini] ||
      [
        model: "gemini-3.1-flash-lite-preview"
      ]
  end

  @doc false
  def build_request_payload(messages, tools, config, opts) do
    # Convert messages to Gemini format
    # user request: ignore system instructions and pass full profile as user text
    gemini_contents = convert_messages_to_gemini(messages)
    
    enable_thinking = opts[:enable_thinking] || config[:enable_thinking] || false
    
    generation_config = %{
      "temperature" => opts[:temperature] || 1.0,
      "maxOutputTokens" => opts[:max_tokens] || config[:max_tokens] || 16000
    }
    
    generation_config = if enable_thinking do
      Map.put(generation_config, "thinkingConfig", %{"includeThoughts" => true})
    else
      generation_config
    end

    # Gemini requires at least one message in contents.
    # If empty (unlikely with system->user conversion), inject dummy.
    gemini_contents = if gemini_contents == [] do
      [%{"role" => "user", "parts" => [%{"text" => "Please proceed."}]}]
    else
      gemini_contents
    end

    base_payload = %{
      "contents" => gemini_contents,
      "generationConfig" => generation_config
    }
    
    # Add tools if provided
    if tools != nil and tools != [] do
      # Smart mode selection based on tool purpose:
      # - Use ANY for strict functional tools (must call): legislation, verdict, blueprint, identity
      # - Use AUTO for planning/reasoning tools: allows model to generate content first
      tool_names = Enum.map(tools, fn t -> t["name"] || t[:name] end)
      strict_tools = ["submit_legislation", "submit_verdict", "submit_blueprint", "mkdir", "touch", 
                      "set_node_identity", "submit_chronicle", "write_to_file", "submit_plan"]
      mode = opts[:tool_choice] || (if Enum.any?(tool_names, fn name -> name in strict_tools end), do: "ANY", else: "AUTO")
      
      base_payload
      |> Map.put("tools", [%{"functionDeclarations" => wrap_tools(tools)}])
      |> Map.put("toolConfig", %{"functionCallingConfig" => %{"mode" => mode}})
    else
      base_payload
    end
  end

  @doc false
  def convert_messages_to_gemini(messages) do
    # Gemini uses "user" and "model" roles.
    # We treat "system" messages as "user" messages to force them into the conversation flow
    # instead of using the separate systemInstruction field which can weak-frame the model.
    
    Enum.map(messages, fn msg ->
      role = msg[:role] || msg["role"]
      content = msg[:content] || msg["content"]
      
      gemini_role = case role do
        "assistant" -> "model"
        "tool" -> "function"
        "system" -> "user"  # FORCE SYSTEM TO USER
        _ -> "user"
      end
      
      # Handle tool calls in assistant messages
      tool_calls = msg[:tool_calls] || msg["tool_calls"]
      
      parts = cond do
        tool_calls && length(tool_calls) > 0 ->
          # Convert tool calls to Gemini function call format
          Enum.map(tool_calls, fn call ->
            func = call["function"]
            # Ensure args are a map
            args = if is_binary(func["arguments"]) do
               case Jason.decode(func["arguments"]) do
                  {:ok, decoded} -> decoded
                  _ -> %{} # Fallback
               end
            else
               func["arguments"]
            end
            
            %{
              "functionCall" => %{
                "name" => func["name"],
                "args" => args
              }
            }
          end)
        
        msg[:name] || msg["name"] ->
          # This is a tool response
          [%{
            "functionResponse" => %{
              "name" => msg[:name] || msg["name"],
              "response" => %{
                "content" => content
              }
            }
          }]
        
        true ->
          # Regular text content
          [%{"text" => content}]
      end
      
      %{
        "role" => gemini_role,
        "parts" => parts
      }
    end)
  end

  @doc false
  def wrap_tools(tools) do
    Enum.map(tools, fn tool ->
      %{
        "name" => tool["name"] || tool[:name],
        "description" => tool["description"] || tool[:description],
        "parameters" => tool["parameters"] || tool[:parameters]
      }
    end)
  end

  @doc false
  def parse_response({:error, _} = error), do: error
  def parse_response(body) do
    # Handle both single response (Map) and streaming response (List)
    # This prevents the ArgumentError when using streamGenerateContent without stream handling.
    response_data = if is_list(body), do: List.first(body), else: body
    
    candidates = response_data["candidates"]
    
    if is_nil(candidates) or length(candidates) == 0 do
      Logger.error("[GeminiAdapter] No candidates in response: #{inspect(body)}")
      {:error, :invalid_response}
    else
      candidate = List.first(candidates)
      content_data = candidate["content"]
      parts = content_data["parts"] || []
      finish_reason = candidate["finishReason"]
      
      # Extract text content, thinking, and function calls
      {text_content, thinking_content, tool_calls} = extract_content_and_tools(parts)
      
      # Debug logging
      log_response(text_content, tool_calls, thinking_content)

      # Build response struct
      response = %Response{
        content: text_content,
        thinking: thinking_content,
        tool_calls: tool_calls,
        finish_reason: finish_reason,
        token_usage: parse_token_usage(response_data)
      }

      {:ok, response}
    end
  end
  
  defp extract_content_and_tools(parts) do
    text_parts = Enum.filter(parts, fn part -> Map.has_key?(part, "text") end)
    function_parts = Enum.filter(parts, fn part -> Map.has_key?(part, "functionCall") end)
    thought_parts = Enum.filter(parts, fn part -> 
      Map.has_key?(part, "thought") or Map.has_key?(part, "reasoning_content") 
    end)
    
    text_content = if length(text_parts) > 0 do
      Enum.map_join(text_parts, "\n", fn part -> part["text"] end)
    else
      ""
    end

    thinking_content = if length(thought_parts) > 0 do
      Enum.map_join(thought_parts, "\n", fn part -> 
        part["thought"] || part["reasoning_content"] 
      end)
    else
      nil
    end
    
    tool_calls = if length(function_parts) > 0 do
      Enum.map(function_parts, fn part ->
        func_call = part["functionCall"]
        %{
          "id" => "call_#{:erlang.unique_integer([:positive])}",
          "type" => "function",
          "function" => %{
            "name" => func_call["name"],
            "arguments" => Jason.encode!(func_call["args"])
          }
        }
      end)
    else
      nil
    end
    
    {text_content, thinking_content, tool_calls}
  end

  defp parse_token_usage(body) do
    usage = body["usageMetadata"]
    
    if usage do
      %{
        prompt_tokens: usage["promptTokenCount"] || 0,
        completion_tokens: usage["candidatesTokenCount"] || 0,
        total: usage["totalTokenCount"] || 0
      }
    else
      %{total: 0}
    end
  end

  defp log_request(payload, tools, config) do
    IO.puts("\n\n🟢 [Gemini Input] ================================================")
    IO.puts("Model: #{config[:model] || "gemini-3.1-flash-lite-preview"}")
    IO.puts("Endpoint: Google Gemini API")
    
    if tools != nil and tools != [] do
      tool_names = Enum.map_join(tools, ", ", &(&1["name"] || &1[:name]))
      IO.puts("✅ Function Calling Enabled: #{length(tools)} tools (#{tool_names})")
    else
      IO.puts("⚠️ No tools provided")
    end

    if payload["systemInstruction"] do
      IO.puts("📜 System Instruction:")
      IO.puts(get_in(payload, ["systemInstruction", "parts", Access.at(0), "text"]))
    end

    contents = payload["contents"] || []
    IO.puts("--- Messages Sent (#{length(contents)}) ---")
    Enum.each(contents, fn msg ->
      role = String.upcase(msg["role"] || "UNKNOWN")
      parts = msg["parts"] || []
      
      # Extract text from parts
      text = parts
        |> Enum.filter(&Map.has_key?(&1, "text"))
        |> Enum.map_join(" ", &(&1["text"]))
      
      if text != "" do
        IO.puts("[#{role}]: #{text}")
      end
    end)
    IO.puts("==================================================================\n")
  end

  defp log_response(content, tool_calls, thinking) do
    IO.puts("\n\n🟣 [Gemini Output] ===============================================")

    if thinking && thinking != "" do
      IO.puts("💭 Reasoning:")
      IO.puts(thinking)
      IO.puts("------------------------------------------------------------------")
    end

    if tool_calls && length(tool_calls) > 0 do
      IO.puts("🔧 Tool Calls Detected (#{length(tool_calls)}):")
      Enum.each(tool_calls, fn call ->
        func = call["function"]
        IO.puts("  - #{func["name"]}(#{func["arguments"]})")
      end)
    end
    
    if content && content != "" do
      IO.puts("📝 Content:")
      IO.puts(content)
    end
    
    IO.puts("==================================================================\n")
  end
end

------------------------------------------------------------
File: serving.ex

defmodule ACN.AI.LLM.Serving do
  @moduledoc """
  The Hippocampus: Hosts the Tiny-Agent 1.5B model.
  Serves as the cognitive engine for ACN.
  """
  require Logger

  @repo_id "driaforall/Tiny-Agent-a-1.5B"
  @serving_name ACN.LocalLLM

  def child_spec(opts) do
    %{
      id: __MODULE__,
      start: {__MODULE__, :start_link, [opts]},
      type: :worker,
      restart: :permanent
    }
  end

  def start_link(_opts \\ []) do
    Logger.info("Starting ACN.AI.LLM.Serving (The Hippocampus)...")
    Logger.info("Loading model from #{@repo_id}. This may take a while...")

    # 1. Load Model & Tokenizer
    # Qwen2 is Llama-compatible, forcing Llama module if Qwen2 not found
    {:ok, model_info} = Bumblebee.load_model({:hf, @repo_id}, module: Bumblebee.Text.Llama, architecture: :for_causal_language_modeling)
    {:ok, tokenizer} = Bumblebee.load_tokenizer({:hf, @repo_id}, module: Bumblebee.Text.LlamaTokenizer)
    {:ok, generation_config} = Bumblebee.load_generation_config({:hf, @repo_id})

    # 2. Configure Generation
    # Strategy: Multinomial, top_p 0.95, temp 0.1 (low temp for precision)
    # Max tokens: 4096
    generation_config =
      Bumblebee.configure(generation_config,
        max_new_tokens: 4096,
        strategy: %{
          type: :multinomial_sampling,
          top_p: 0.95
        },
        temperature: 0.1
      )

    # 3. Create Serving with EXLA Compilation
    # Batch Size 1, Sequence Length 4096 for high-context single-user
    serving =
      Bumblebee.Text.generation(model_info, tokenizer, generation_config,
        compile: [
          batch_size: 1,
          sequence_length: 4096
        ],
        defn_options: [compiler: EXLA]
      )

    Logger.info("Model loaded and serving compiled. Starting Nx.Serving process under name #{inspect(@serving_name)}...")

    # 4. Start Serving Process
    Nx.Serving.start_link(name: @serving_name, serving: serving)
  end
end

defmodule ACN.AI.Embedder.Serving do
  @moduledoc """
  Top-level supervision for the Bumblebee Serving process.
  Loads the 'all-MiniLM-L6-v2' model and exposes it as an Nx.Serving.
  """
  require Logger

  @model_repo "sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2"

  def start_link(_opts) do
     Logger.info("Booting Visual Cortex (Bumblebee)...")

     # Load Model & Tokenizer
     {:ok, model_info} = Bumblebee.load_model({:hf, @model_repo})
     {:ok, tokenizer} = Bumblebee.load_tokenizer({:hf, @model_repo})

     # Create Serving
     serving = Bumblebee.Text.text_embedding(model_info, tokenizer,
       output_pool: :mean_pooling,
       output_attribute: :hidden_state,
       compile: [batch_size: 8, sequence_length: 64],
       defn_options: [compiler: EXLA]
     )

     # Start Serving Process (Global Name)
     Nx.Serving.start_link(name: ACN.Embedder, serving: serving, batch_size: 8, batch_timeout: 100)
  end

  # Nx.Serving.start_link handles the process.
  # We wrap in a Supervisor if we want more control, but for now we'll put this module in the tree directly
  # Or rather, we use this module to Init and Return the child spec.

  def child_spec(opts) do
    %{
      id: __MODULE__,
      start: {__MODULE__, :start_link, [opts]},
      type: :worker
    }
  end
end

defmodule Mix.Tasks.Acn.Rescue do
  use Mix.Task
  require Logger

  @shortdoc "Rescues (unfreezes) an ACN node and resets LiveWire stress."

  @moduledoc """
  Rescues an ACN node by sending a :rescue cast to its controller and the global live_wire.

  Usage:
      mix acn.rescue --node <node_id>

  Example:
      mix acn.rescue --node sqrt_endpoint
  """

  def run(args) do
    # Start the necessary applications
    Mix.Task.run("app.start")

    {opts, _, _} = OptionParser.parse(args, switches: [node: :string])

    node_id = opts[:node] || "root"

    IO.puts("==> Rescuing ACN System...")

    # 1. Rescue LiveWire (reset global stress)
    IO.puts("    - Resetting LiveWire stress...")
    ACN.Router.route(:rescue, {:local, :live_wire}, %ACN.Router.State{node_id: "root"})

    # 2. Rescue specific Controller
    if node_id != "root" do
      IO.puts("    - Unfreezing Controller for node: #{node_id}...")
      ACN.Router.route(:rescue, {:local, :controller}, %ACN.Router.State{node_id: node_id})
    else
        IO.puts("    - Unfreezing Root Controller...")
        ACN.Router.route(:rescue, {:local, :controller}, %ACN.Router.State{node_id: "root"})
    end

    IO.puts("==> Rescue signals sent successfully.")
  end
end


------------------------------------------------------------
File: genesis.ex
------------------------------------------------------------
defmodule Mix.Tasks.Acn.Genesis do
  use Mix.Task
  require Logger

  @shortdoc "Ignites the ACN organism (The Big Bang)"

  @moduledoc """
  Starts the ACN system and spawns the first root node.

  ## Usage

      mix acn.genesis --task "Build a Todo App"

  ## Options

    * `--task` - The task description for the root node (required)
  """

  @impl Mix.Task
  def run(args) do
    # Parse arguments
    {opts, _, _} = OptionParser.parse(args, strict: [task: :string])

    task_description = opts[:task] || raise "Missing required --task argument"

    Mix.shell().info("=== ACN GENESIS ===")
    Mix.shell().info("Task: #{task_description}")

    # 1. Start the application (starts NodeSupervisor, LiveWire, Messenger)
    Mix.Task.run("app.start")

    # Wait for infrastructure to stabilize
    Process.sleep(1000)
    Mix.shell().info("Core infrastructure initialized.")

    # 2. The First Spark: Spawn Root Node via NodeSupervisor
    Mix.shell().info("Spawning Root Node...")
 
    # Use consistent 'root' ID for the primary node
    node_id = "root"

    workspace_root = ACN.Infrastructure.Workspace.root_path()
    # Ensure the workspace root exists, but let the identity bot choose the subfolder
    File.mkdir_p!(workspace_root)
    
    # SPECIFICATION FIDELITY: Always treat the task description as the original specification for 1:1 fidelity
    word_count = task_description |> String.split(~r/\s+/) |> length()
    
    {original_spec, spec_metadata, effective_task_desc} = if word_count >= 0 do
      Mix.shell().info("📋 Applying specification fidelity mode for all tasks (#{word_count} words)")
      
      # Extract a concise task summary for Pillar V
      [first_line | _] = String.split(task_description, "\n")
      summary = "Implement System defined in Specification: #{String.slice(first_line, 0, 100)}... (See Pillar VII)"
      
      {
        task_description,
        %{
          version: "1.0",
          ingested_at: DateTime.utc_now(),
          source: "genesis_command",
          word_count: word_count
        },
        summary
      }
    else
      Mix.shell().info("🎯 Standard task description (#{word_count} words) - using normal mode")
      {nil, nil, task_description}
    end
 
    node_args = %{
      task: node_id,
      task_description: effective_task_desc,
      parent_node: nil,  # Root has no parent
      genesis_flag: true,
      path: "root",   # Signal to Supervisor to use workspace_root and let Bot extend it
      context: %{
        env_config: %{
          workspace_root: workspace_root
        }
      },
      # SPECIFICATION FIDELITY: Add specification fields for root node
      original_specification: original_spec,
      specification_depth: 0,  # Root is always depth 0
      specification_metadata: spec_metadata
    }

    case ACN.Core.NodeSupervisor.start_child_node(node_args) do
      {:ok, child_pid} ->
        Mix.shell().info("✓ Child Node spawned: #{inspect(child_pid)}")

        # Wait for the Root Enclave to boot its children
        Process.sleep(2000)

        # Initialize Governance on the Controller
        # Get Controller PID using SCOPED name
        controller_id = "#{node_id}_controller"
        controller_pid = ACN.Core.NodeRegistry.whereis_name({:controller, controller_id})
 
        if is_pid(controller_pid) do
          Mix.shell().info("✓ Governance initialized for #{node_id} (Controller: #{inspect(controller_pid)})")
        else
          Mix.shell().error("! Controller (#{controller_id}) not found - Governance may fail initialization.")
        end

        # Verify other local agents
        verify_node_agents(node_id)

        # Initialize the Root Supervisor Node
        GenServer.cast(child_pid, {:init_node, node_args})
        Mix.shell().info("✓ Root Node initialized")

        Mix.shell().info("✓ Genesis complete. The organism is alive.")
        Mix.shell().info("")
        Mix.shell().info("Dashboard: http://172.20.68.58:5051")
        Mix.shell().info("Node ID: #{node_id}")
        Mix.shell().info("Press Ctrl+C to stop.")

        # 3. Keep the task running until the Root Node finishes or exits.
        # FIX: The root supervisor intentionally stays alive after task completion,
        # so a bare :DOWN monitor never fires. Subscribe to the PubSub global topic
        # so {:genesis_complete, ^node_id} unblocks us when the task truly finishes.
        Phoenix.PubSub.subscribe(ACN.PubSub, "acn:monitor:global")
        ref = Process.monitor(child_pid)

        wait_for_completion(node_id, ref)

      {:error, reason} ->
        Logger.error("✗ Failed to spawn root node: #{inspect(reason)}")
        # Add a delay and flush to ensure we see the error
        Logger.flush()
        Process.sleep(500)
        System.halt(1)
    end
  end

  defp wait_for_completion(node_id, ref) do
    receive do
      {:genesis_complete, ^node_id, working_path} ->
        Mix.shell().info("✓ Task complete. Root organism '#{node_id}' finished successfully.")
        Mix.shell().info("Codebase: #{working_path}")
        # Explicitly exit the task after printing
        System.stop(0)

      {:genesis_complete, other_id, _path} ->
        Mix.shell().info("Received genesis_complete for '#{other_id}' (expected '#{node_id}'). Continuing to wait...")
        wait_for_completion(node_id, ref)

      {:DOWN, ^ref, :process, _object, reason} ->
        Mix.shell().info("Root Node process exited: #{inspect(reason)}")
        System.stop(0)
    end
  end

  defp verify_node_agents(node_id) do
    roles = [:live_wire, :messenger, :task_manager, :actuator]
    Enum.each(roles, fn role ->
      id = "#{node_id}_#{role}"
      pid = ACN.Core.NodeRegistry.whereis_name({role, id})
      if is_pid(pid) do
        Mix.shell().info("#{role}: Active (#{inspect(pid)})")
      else
        Mix.shell().error("#{role} (#{id}): Not found in NodeRegistry")
      end
    end)
  end
end

defmodule Mix.Tasks.Acn.InitMemory do
  use Mix.Task
  require Logger

  @shortdoc "Initializes connection to ACMS"

  def run(_) do
    Mix.Task.run("app.start")
    Logger.info("Checking ACMS connection...")

    case ACN.Infrastructure.MemoryClient.check_connection() do
      :ok ->
        IO.puts("✓ ACMS is online and healthy")
      {:error, reason} ->
        IO.puts("✗ ACMS connection failed: #{inspect reason}")
        IO.puts("  Make sure ACMS is running at http://localhost:4000")
    end
  end
end

------------------------------------------------------------
File: acn_overview.md
------------------------------------------------------------
# Project ACN (Autonomous Control Network)

ACN is a sophisticated, distributed, and hierarchical AI agent framework built on Elixir/OTP. It provides a secure, observable, and scalable environment for multiple AI agents to collaborate on complex tasks through hierarchical decomposition and sovereign execution.

## Core Architecture
ACN operates on a **Node-based hierarchy**:
- **Root Node**: The starting point of an organism (task execution).
- **Intermediate Nodes**: Planners that decompose high-level tasks into smaller batches of sub-tasks.
- **Leaf Nodes**: Workers that execute specific, atomic implementations (e.g., writing code, running commands).

### Agent Roles (Per Node Enclave)
Each node contains a dedicated set of agents:
- **SupervisorAgent**: Manages the node lifecycle, identity (Intermediate vs. Leaf), and workspace isolation.
- **Controller**: The node's "brain." It manages a state machine (Legislation -> Architecting -> Execution -> Validation). It uses AI to generate "blueprints" and "rules" (Legislation).
- **TaskManager**: Exclusive to Intermediate nodes. It acts as the planner, using AI to generate a Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) of tasks.
- **Actuator**: The node's "hands." It executes physical tools (filesystem, shell, hardware) and AI-powered implementation/remediation.
- **Messenger**: The "chronicler." It batches node events and uses AI to generate narrative summaries of the node's progress.
- **LiveWire**: The "nervous system." It monitors system stress and "pain" signals (failures). High stress triggers throttling or system freezes.

## Key Subsystems

### 1. HSG (Hierarchical System Graph) Observability
A multi-layered telemetry system (the **Panopticon**) that captures every state transition:
- **Phase 0 (Payloads)**: `MicroDBC` (Universal Telemetry Payload) and `TraceContext` (Causal Tracing).
- **Phase 1 (Emission)**: Zero-contention emission from agent processes.
- **Phase 2 (In-Memory Graph)**: Real-time `:digraph` visualization of agent relationships and intents.
- **Phase 3 (Persistence)**: Distributed Mnesia database for forensic auditing.
- **Phase 4 (Spatial Introspection)**: `.shadow` directories in the workspace mirroring the physical file structure with cognitive/autonomic metadata.

### 2. ACMS (Associative Cognitive Memory System)
A semantic memory backend (external service) that stores and retrieves "wisdom"—historical facts, code patterns, and Digital Birth Certificates (DBCs). This allows agents to learn from past executions and avoid redundant work.

### 3. Sovereign Seal (Security)
A cryptographic authorization system:
- **RSA Keypairs**: Each node generates ephemeral keys.
- **JWT Tokens**: Controllers mint scoped tokens (e.g., `fs_write`, `exec_cmd`) that Actuators must verify before performing any physical action.
- **Workspace Jailing**: Strict path enforcement ensuring agents only operate within their assigned `tmp/acn_workspaces` directory.

### 4. AI Pipeline & Profiles
Sophisticated integration with LLMs (Bumblebee, Ollama, OpenAI) using:
- **Context Buffers**: Managed chat history and system prompts.
- **Profiles (EEx Templates)**: Dynamically rendered prompts that inject "Pillars of Reality" (Rules, Worksheets, Wisdom, Tasks).
- **Automated Validation & Remediation**: Controllers use "Judge" bots to verify outputs and trigger "Remediator" bots if a contract is violated.

## Technical Stack
- **Language**: Elixir 1.14+ (OTP, Phoenix, PubSub, Mnesia).
- **AI/ML**: NX, Bumblebee, EXLA.
- **Native**: Rustler (for Firecracker/NIF integrations).
- **Communication**: Distributed Erlang (RPC) + Phoenix PubSub.
- **Security**: Joken/JOSE (JWT/RSA), TokenAuthority.

---

# Analysis of ACN (Autonomous Control Network)

ACN is a sophisticated, distributed, and hierarchical AI agent framework built on Elixir/OTP. It provides a secure, observable, and scalable environment for multiple AI agents to collaborate on complex software engineering tasks through hierarchical decomposition and sovereign execution.

## 1. Core Architecture: The Node Hierarchy

ACN operates on a **Node-based hierarchy**, recursively decomposing goals into sub-tasks:
- **Root Node**: The top-level entry point that initiates an "Organism."
- **Intermediate Nodes (Planners)**: Decompose high-level tasks into smaller, manageable batches of sub-tasks. They act as the "middle management" that spawns child nodes.
- **Leaf Nodes (Workers)**: Atomic execution units that perform specific implementations (e.g., writing code, running tests, or executing shell commands).

Each node is implemented as an **Enclave**, which is an isolated supervisor tree of specialized agents.

## 2. Specialized Agent Roles

Within each node enclave, the following agents collaborate:
- **SupervisorAgent**: Manages the node lifecycle, identity (Intermediate vs. Leaf), and performs "Context Hydration" to aggregate rules and data from parent nodes.
- **Controller**: The node's "brain." It manages the state machine (Legislation -> Architecting -> Execution -> Validation -> Finalization). It generates the node's technical "blueprint."
- **TaskManager**: Exclusive to Intermediate nodes. It acts as the planner, using AI to generate a Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) of tasks.
- **Actuator**: The node's "hands." It executes physical tools (filesystem, shell, hardware) and uses AI for implementation and remediation.
- **Messenger**: The "chronicler." It generates narrative summaries of the node's progress.
- **LiveWire**: The "nervous system." It monitors system stress and failure signals, triggering throttling or system freezes when needed.

## 3. The Node State Machine & Workflow

Each node progresses through a standardized lifecycle:
1.  **Legislation**: AI generates technical specifications (Blueprint) and local rules (Governance).
2.  **Architecting / Delegation**: 
    - Intermediate nodes delegate to the `TaskManager` for planning.
    - Leaf nodes delegate to the `Actuator` for implementation.
3.  **Execution** (Leaf Nodes): The `Actuator` performs physical actions (e.g., `fs_write`, `exec_cmd`).
4.  **Validation**: The `Controller` acts as a "Judge" to verify the output against the blueprint and rules.
5.  **Finalization**: The node aggregates its results (Digital Birth Certificates - DBCs) and performs an "Upstream Merge" to notify its parent of success.

## 4. Key Subsystems & Technical Foundations

- **Sovereign Seal (Security)**: Cryptographic authorization (RSA/JWT) and strict "Spatial Jailing" to isolate workspaces. Each node has its own ephemeral keypair.
- **HSG (Hierarchical System Graph) / Panopticon (Observability)**: A multi-layered telemetry system that captures every state transition, cognitive thought, physical action, and security decision into an in-memory graph (ETS/Digraph) and a distributed database (Mnesia).
- **ACMS (Associative Cognitive Memory System)**: A semantic memory backend for storing and retrieving "wisdom" (historical facts and patterns).
- **AI Pipeline**: Modular integration with AI models (Ollama, TinyAgent, etc.) for autonomous decision-making at every stage.
- **Workspace Management**: Uses `ACN.Infrastructure.Workspace` for isolated, atomic file operations with automatic backups and history tracking.

## 5. Technical Stack

- **Language**: Elixir / Erlang (OTP)
- **Database**: Mnesia (Distributed DB)
- **Web**: Phoenix (Dashboard / PubSub)
- **Native**: Rustler (Firecracker NIFs)
- **AI/ML**: Bumblebee, EXLA, Nx
- **Networking**: Finch (HTTP Client), Phoenix PubSub
- **Hardware**: Circuits GPIO (Nerves integration)

## Conclusion

ACN is more than just an AI agent; it is an autonomous, self-organizing system designed for the highest engineering standards. Its combination of hierarchical task decomposition, cryptographic security, and extreme observability makes it a robust platform for complex, distributed software engineering.

# ACN (Autonomic Computing Network) Architectural Summary

The ACN system is a distributed, agent-based platform designed for autonomous task execution and code remediation. It leverages Elixir/OTP for high availability, fault tolerance, and distributed process management.

## 1. System Core & Infrastructure

### 1.1 Application Startup (`ACN.Application`)
The system follows a strict startup sequence:
- **Infrastructure First**: `Finch` (HTTP), `ClusterManager` (Mnesia/Cluster), `StateBackup`, `NetworkMonitor`, `ACMSLimiter`, and `AuditBuffer`.
- **Registry & Monitoring**: `NodeRegistry`, `LatticeRegistry`, `NodeMonitor`, and `NodeSupervisor`.
- **Communication**: `Phoenix.PubSub` for inter-agent and system-wide messaging.
- **Observability**: `Schema` and `Panopticon` for tracing and monitoring.
- **AI Services**: `TaskSupervisor` and optional model serving.

### 1.2 Distributed Management (`ClusterManager` & `NodeSupervisor`)
- **`ClusterManager`**: Manages Mnesia schema and cluster membership. It includes a sophisticated "least-stressed node" selection algorithm for process placement, using RPC calls to poll metrics from remote nodes via `LiveWire`.
- **`NodeSupervisor`**: A `DynamicSupervisor` that handles local or remote (via RPC) spawning of `SupervisorAgent` (and subsequently `EnclaveSupervisor`).

## 2. Agent Architecture

### 2.1 The Enclave Model (`EnclaveSupervisor`)
Each logical "node" or task workspace is isolated within an "Enclave".
- **`SupervisorAgent`**: Orchestrates the enclave's lifecycle, managing context hydration and hierarchical lineage.
- **`EnclaveSupervisor`**: A transient supervisor managing the core agent suite for a specific node:
    - **`Controller`**: Manages governance, lifecycle, and identity.
    - **`TaskManager`**: Handles task decomposition and management.
    - **`Actuator`**: Executes tasks (filesystem, AI-driven remediation).
    - **`Messenger`**: Manages event buffering and periodic logging.
    - **`LiveWire`**: Handles real-time monitoring and metric collection.

### 2.2 Cognitive Pipeline (`ACN.AI.Pipeline`)
The system uses a ReAct-style loop:
- **`Pipeline.run/4`**: Orchestrates Prompt -> LLM -> Tool -> Execution -> Result.
- **Context Management**: `ContextBuffer` maintains conversation history.
- **Tool Execution**: Tools are looked up in a registry and executed with optional environment state.

## 3. Governance & Security
- **Master Rules (`ACN.Core.Governance.MasterRules`)**: Immutable "Constitution" defining safety, security (no hardcoded secrets), privacy (no external network), and containment (workspace restriction).
- **Token Authority**: Manages security tokens for authorized execution.
- **State Backup**: Provides persistence for agent states across failures.

## 4. Observability & Logging
- **`Panopticon` & `AuditBuffer`**: Provide granular tracing and auditing.
- **Multi-layered Logging**: Separate Mnesia-backed logs for autonomic, physical, cognitive, and security events.

## 5. Persistence
- **Mnesia**: Used for distributed registry (`acn_node_registry_v2`) and event logging.
- **Filesystem**: Used for state backups and permanent logs.

# ACN: Autonomous Control Network — System Analysis

## Overview
The **Autonomous Control Network (ACN)** is a high-fidelity, biologically-inspired AI agent framework built on the Erlang BEAM VM using Elixir. It is designed to solve complex, long-horizon software engineering tasks through a recursive, hierarchical node architecture that emphasizes extreme observability, security, and robustness.

---

## Core Architectural Concepts

### 1. Hierarchical Recursive Nodes
The system operates as an organism of nodes. Each node represents a specific level of task abstraction:
- **Root Node**: The entry point of a task (Genesis).
- **Intermediate Nodes**: High-level task planners that decompose requirements into sub-tasks.
- **Leaf Nodes**: Execution units that perform actual coding or filesystem operations.

### 2. The Multi-Agent Node Structure
Every node in the ACN contains a specialized "enclave" of agents that work together:
- **SupervisorAgent**: The lifecycle manager of the node. It handles initialization, identity determination (Leaf vs. Intermediate), and monitors all other agents within its enclave.
- **Controller**: The node's "brain." It operates in phases:
    - **Legislation**: Uses AI to create a technical blueprint and local safety rules.
    - **Architecture**: Decides whether to delegate to a TaskManager (Intermediate) or an Actuator (Leaf).
    - **Judge**: A validation phase where an AI "Judge" verifies that the output matches the blueprint and rules.
- **TaskManager**: The "planner" for intermediate nodes. It uses AI to break down a task into sequential batches of parallel child nodes.
- **Actuator**: The "hands" of the node. It executes filesystem writes, patches, and commands. It also runs the "Code Drafter" and "Remediator" AI models.
- **LiveWire**: The "nervous system." It monitors stress levels and pain signals, triggering alerts or freezes if the system encounters terminal failures.

---

## HSG: High-level Supervisor Group (Observability Layer)
The ACN features a sophisticated observability layer called **HSG**, which treats system telemetry as a "Digital Nervous System."

### The Five Vectors
All system events are categorized into five relational vectors:
1. **Lattice (The Spine)**: Tracks phase transitions and the causal backbone of the entire task tree.
2. **Cognitive (The Brain)**: Records AI interaction metadata (models, tokens, prompts).
3. **Physical (The Hands)**: Logs every filesystem mutation with unified diffs and checksums.
4. **Security (The Immune System)**: Monitors JWT minting and authorization checks.
5. **Autonomic (The Nervous System)**: Tracks health metrics and stress levels.

### Cryptographic Integrity
Events are stored in **Mnesia** with a cryptographic hash chain (`hash` and `prev_hash`). This ensures a verifiable, forensic history of the agent's actions from genesis to completion.

---

## Task Lifecycle (The "Big Bang" to Completion)
1. **Genesis**: A task is ignited via a Mix task (`mix acn.genesis`).
2. **Bootstrap**: A Root Supervisor is spawned, and a `ContextFetcher` hydrates the node with historical wisdom and technical rules.
3. **Legislation**: The Controller uses an LLM to "legislate" a technical specification (Blueprint) for the task.
4. **Planning (Intermediate)**: The TaskManager generates a DAG of sub-tasks.
5. **Execution (Leaf)**: The Actuator writes code or executes commands based on the blueprint.
6. **Validation**: The Judge bot verifies the work. If it fails, the **Remediator** bot is triggered to fix the issues.
7. **Merge**: Results are merged back to the parent node's "Worksheet" (memory).

---

## Technology Stack
- **Language**: Elixir (1.14+)
- **Runtime**: Erlang OTP (BEAM)
- **Database**: Mnesia (Distributed, ACID)
- **AI/ML**: Bumblebee, EXLA, Nx (Tensors and Embeddings)
- **Native**: Rustler (Rust NIFs for high-performance sandboxing)
- **Web**: Phoenix Framework (Real-time Panopticon Dashboard)
- **Infrastructure**: circuits_gpio (Hardware interaction)

---

## Key Artifacts and Directories
- `lib/acn/agents/`: Core agent logic (Supervisor, Controller, etc.).
- `lib/acn/observability/`: HSG telemetry and Mnesia schema.
- `lib/acn/infrastructure/`: Workspace management, memory clients, and sandboxing.
- `priv/profiles/`: YAML definitions for AI bot personas and prompts.
- `agents/`: (Optional) Root-level agent definitions.
- `.shadow/`: A spatial introspection layer mirroring the workspace with telemetry metadata.
